# Tests/GameLogicTests/KnownOutcomes.txt
#
# The twins' outcomes (D33). Beside each of the twins' known answers (KnownAnswers.txt), the outcomes of the same state:
# the named facts of the game's state a player could notice, as Elite::OutcomeText writes them (GameLogic/Outcome.h), each
# read through Elite::OutcomeValue, the one function that knows where each lives. They were recorded from the native code
# in a run whose every state digest met the original's, so each is the original's.
#
# Format: one answer's outcomes per line, the twin's name, the ordinal and the outcomes, separated by single spaces:
#
#   TWIN ORDINAL NAME=VALUE NAME=VALUE ...
#
#   TWIN        the name the twin is made with, TwinRig rig("TwinTitle")
#   ORDINAL     the ordinal of the known answer the outcomes stand beside, which is the same state
#   NAME=VALUE  one for each of Elite::OUTCOMES, in its order: a number in decimal; cargo, the 17 commodities held, in the
#               market's order, separated by commas; title, commanderRankText's nine characters
#
# Lines are sorted by twin and ordinal. Blank lines and lines that start with '#' are ignored.
#
# Checking: a TwinRig checks the outcomes beside each known answer it checks. An outcome of the player's progress
# (Elite::OutcomeKind::Progress: where the commander is, what they hold and have fitted, their standing and the missions)
# that differs fails the twin. A timing outcome (energy, the shields, speed, fuel, the ship slots in use, the temperatures,
# the altitude and the missions' frame counters), which moves with the exact timing of flight, is reported: the test's log
# names the twin, the ordinal and both values, and each twin logs how many it checked and how many differ. Missing
# outcomes fail the twin, naming it and the ordinal, and so does a name the recorded outcomes lack.
#
# Recording: with OUTPOST_ELITE_RECORD_GUARD set, in one process and never in CI, each twin records its outcomes instead,
# replacing its own lines, and only if every state digest it gave met its known answer. A second recording reproduces
# this file byte for byte. A twin that is deleted takes its lines with it.
#
